Function internal_score(a):
  a = np.maximum(a,0); s=a.sum(); if s<0.01: inf; a=a/s; c=np.convolve(a,a); return 2*len(a)*c.max()
We can use same but evaluator clamps; we keep nonnegative.

Let's design optimizer functions:

```
def _norm1(a):
  a=np.asarray(a,float)
  a=np.nan_to_num(a)
  a=np.clip(a,0,1e6)
  s=a.sum()
  if s<1e-15: return np.ones_like(a)/len(a)
  return a/s
```

```
def optimize_log(a0, time_limit, rng, ...):
  y=np.log(np.maximum(a0,1e-12)); y-=y.mean()
  mzero... 
  best=a0, best_sc
  lr=0.15
  for it:
    if time > limit: break
    a=softmax(y)
    c=conv(a)
    sc=...
    update best
    compute ga
    # normalize gradient: ga / (max|ga|)
    gz = a * (ga - dot)
    # Scale gradient by 1/(m*maxc?) to make O(1)
    scale = np.linalg.norm(gz)
    if scale>0: gz/=scale
    # Adam
    step = lr * mhat/(sqrt+v)
    # Backtracking: try full step, half...
    y_new = y; base_sc = best_sc? We can evaluate a_sc current.
    for alpha in [1,0.5,0.25,0.125]:
       y_trial = y + alpha*step
       a_trial=softmax(y_trial)
       sc_trial=score(a_trial)
       if sc_trial < a_sc - 1e-12 or alpha<0.125: accept
    # If no improvement, maybe accept small exploration with prob? But track best.
```

  a=softmax(y); c=conv(a)
  beta=...
  z=beta*(c - c.max())
  z=clip(z,-50,0)
  logsum = c.max() + np.log(np.sum(exp(z)))

  w=np.exp(z); w/=w.sum()
  ga = _grad_weights(a, beta*w? Wait derivative of LSE wrt c: 2n? Let's derive.

  LSE=cm + (1/beta) log sum exp(beta(c-cm)). dLSE/dc = softmax(beta(c-cm)) = w.

  gz = a*(ga - dot)
  return Phi, gz

Detailed optimizer:

```
def _softmax_y(y):
   z=y-y.max()
   # clip z to [-40,0] to prevent underflow? If some z <-40, a_i=0; could be okay but log variable can't recover if underflow. Use [-50,0].
   z=np.clip(z,-50,0)
   e=np.exp(z)
   s=e.sum()
   return e/s
```
If clip to -50, a_i ~1.9e-22, nonzero. Sum may be dominated by top; okay. But if many -50, sum includes 1e-22, negligible, derivative for those zero; can't increase them because y step can move from -50 upward but after softmax normalization, gradient for clipped? ```
def _run_log_optimizer(a0, t_start, t_end, rng, beta_init=10, beta_end=100, mu_init=0.8, mu_end=0.0, lr=0.2, ls=False, label=''):
    y = _log_from_a(a0)
    m = np.zeros_like(y); v=np.zeros_like(y); t=0
    best=a0.copy(); best_sc=_score(best)
    no_imp=0; it=0; last_print? no
    while time < t_end - 0.5:
       it+=1
       y = _center_clip(y)
       a=softmax(y)
       c=conv(a); cm=c.max(); sc=2*n*cm
       if sc<best_sc: best=...; no_imp=0; lr=min(lr*1.05,0.5)
       else: no_imp+=1
       # compute weights
       frac=(time - t_start)/max(t_end-t_start)
       beta = beta_init + (beta_end-beta_init)*frac? But dynamic based on top gap better.
       use_top = (it % 5 == 0) ? exact? 
       # maybe weights = _max_weights(c, beta, frac)
       w = _max_weights(c, beta, top_mode)
       if mu>0:
          w = w + mu * c  # c sums 1
       ga = _grad_weights(a,w)
       # optional exact subgradient projection: if no improve, use top uniform.
       gz = a*(ga - np.dot(ga,a))
       # normalize? Adam handles. But if ga all zero, break.
       t +=1
       m = 0.9*m +0.1*gz
       v =0.999*v+0.001*gz*gz
       step = lr * (m/(1-0.9**t)) / (np.sqrt(v/(1-0.999**t))+1e-8)
       step = np.clip(step, -0.5, 0.5)
       # line search/backtrack
       # We can evaluate trial for alpha 1, 0.5, 0.25.
       y_trial = y + step; a_trial... sc_trial
       accept? If sc_trial < sc + 1e-10 accept. Else try 0.5...
       If none better, maybe accept 0.25 if sc_trial <= sc + 0.001 (exploration) else skip and reduce lr.
       if not np.all(finite): reset.
       if no_imp > threshold: perturb y = log(best)+noise, reset Adam, lr=...
    return best,best_sc
```

Weights function design:
```
def _active_weights(c, beta, use_top_rank=3, tie_frac=1e-4):
  cm=c.max()
  # Determine top indices where c >= cm - tie_tol
  tie_tol = max(1e-8, tie_frac*cm) # e.g. 1e-4*0.006=6e-7
  active = np.where(c >= cm - tie_tol)[0]
  if len(active)>8: active = top 8 by c
  if len(active)<=2:
      # use soft LSE with dynamic beta
      gap = max(cm - (sorted second if exists else cm), 1e-8*cm)
      b = 6.0/gap
      b = min(max(b, 50.0), 1e6)
      z= b*(c-cm); z=clip(z,-50,0); w=exp(z); w/=w.sum()
  else:
      # smooth among active and maybe soft outside
      # use weights proportional to exp(6*(c-cm)/ (gap_active or tie_tol))
      gap = max(cm - c[active].min(), tie_tol)
      b = 3.0/gap
      z=b*(c-cm); z=clip(z,-50,0); w=exp(z); w/=w.sum()
  return w
```

Direct gradient loop implementation:
```
def _grad_weights(a,w):
    m=a.size
    if m <= 256:
       out=np.empty(m)
       # Use stride? For each j, w[j:j+m]
       for j in range(m):
           out[j]=np.dot(a, w[j:j+m])
       return out*2
    else:
       N=1
       while N < 3*m: N*=2
       A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
       W=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
       h=np.fft.irfft(A*np.conj(W),N)[:m]
       return 2*h
```
Check direct w length exactly 2m-1. If m=1, w length1, loop j=0 w[0:1]. ok.

Score and conv can be optimized by using `np.convolve(a,a)`. For repeated evaluations in line search, okay.

Local polish function:
```
def _polish(a, rng, max_evals=200, time_limit=None):
   best=a.copy(); best_sc=_score(a)
   n=a.size
   evals=0
   for trial in range(max_evals):
      if time: break
      if rng.random()<0.5:
        i,j=rng.integers(0,n,2)
        if i==j: continue
        # sample delta around current a_j fraction
        base = max(1e-6, a[j])
        for sign in (1,-1):
           # maybe scan 5 fractions
           for frac in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,0.8]:
              d=sign*frac*base
              b=best.copy()
              b[i]+=d; b[j]-=d
              if b[i]<0 or b[j]<0: continue
              b/=b.sum()
              sc=_score(b)
              if sc<best_sc: update; base=... ; break
      else:
        L=int(rng.integers(3, max(5,n//5)))
        s=int(rng.integers(0,n-L+1))
        f=np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.2))
        b=best.copy(); b[s:s+L]*=f; b/=b.sum(); sc=...
   return best,best_sc
```
This is expensive but can run few hundred evals. For n=127, 500 evals*16k=8M ops fine. Use at end.

But pair transfer from best with fractions of a[j] may not explore enough. Could also random coordinate scaling of random subset.

Parametric initial generation for any n:
```
def _make_inits(n, rng, parent):
  inits=[]
  def add(x): x=np.maximum(x,0); if sum>0 add normalized
  if parent: add(upsample parent to n)
  x=np.arange(n)
  # uniform
  add(ones)
  # power increasing/decreasing
  for p in [-0.5,-0.2,0.1,0.3,0.6,1.0,1.5,-1.0]: add((x+1)**p)
  # bilinear endpoints
  for p in [-1,-0.5,0,0.5,1]:
    for q in [-1,-0.5,0,0.5,1]:
       add((x+1)**p * (n-x)**q)
  # exponent tilt
  for g in [-2,-1,0.5,1,2]: add(exp(g*x/(n-1)))
  # damped oscillations? nonnegative: 1+0.2 sin(2π f x)
  for f in [1,2,3]: add(1+0.15 sin... +1e-6)
  # random
  for scale in [0.3,1.0]: add(softmax(normal))
  # parent noised
  if parent: add(parent*exp(r.normal(scale=0.1)))
  limit 30
```
But if we spend time evaluating many inits with conv O(n^2), 30*16k=480k trivial.

```
def _upsample(a, n): np.interp(np.linspace(0,1,n), np.linspace(0,1,len(a)), a) then normalize; preserve positive.
```

Weights update:
```
def _make_weights(c, frac, mu, rng=None):
    cm=float(c.max())
    # top gap
    # sort only top few for speed: use argsort? c length <= 511, fine.
    idx=np.argsort(c)[::-1]
    top=c[idx[:3]]
    gap= top[0] - (top[1] if len(top)>1 else top[0])
    gap=max(gap, 1e-9*cm + 1e-15)
    focus = 1.0 + 5.0*frac  # 1 to6
    beta=focus/gap
    # cap beta such that beta*(cm-c.min()) <= 60
    min_c=float(c.min())
    beta=max(1.0, min(beta, 60.0/max(cm-min_c,1e-15)))
    z=np.clip(beta*(c-cm), -60, 0)
    w=np.exp(z); wsum=w.sum()
    if wsum<=0: w=zeros; w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1
    else: w/=wsum
    if mu>0:
       # c sums approx 1 due a normalized; renormalize c part to sum 1
       cs=float(c.sum())
       if cs>0: w = w + mu * (c/cs)
       wsum=w.sum()
       if wsum>0: w/=wsum
    return w
```

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300):
  rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
  t0=time.monotonic()
  deadline=t0+budget_s-12
  # parent
  parent=None; n0=127
  try: ...
  # best global
  best_arr = parent if parent else ones(127)/127
  best_sc = _score(best_arr)
  # quick inits of same n
  inits = _make_inits(n0, rng, parent=parent)
  for a in inits: ... update best
  # allocate main
  main_end = t0 + 0.78*(budget_s-12) maybe
  # Stage 1 soft flat alpha=0.5 lr=0.15 from best until main_end*0.4
  best,best_sc = _run_optimizer(best, t0, stage1_end, rng, lr=0.15, alpha_init=0.6, alpha_end=0.2, focus? )
  # Stage 2 intermediate
  # Stage 3 final pure max with maybe L-BFGS
  # local polish
  # optional lengths if time
  # final validate
```

No-improve reset: The optimizer should keep best inside. If no best improvement for `reset_every` iters (e.g., 500), it perturbs y based on current best with noise scale increasing, resets Adam, resets no_improve. But if the current y is in a bad region not improving best but maybe near , perturb. Also if current score > best_score + 0.05, reset to best. Use time.

We need be careful: `_run_optimizer` returns array normalized. It should evaluate using internal score matching evaluator (nonnegative). It should not modify input.

Let's write `_run_optimizer` code in detail:

```
def _run_optimizer(a0, t_start, t_end, rng, lr_start=0.18, lr_end=0.08, alpha_start=0.6, alpha_end=0.1, focus_start=1.0, focus_end=8.0, cap=0.4, name=''):
    if t_end <= t_start + 1.0: return a0, _score(a0)
    m=a0.size
    y=_log_from_a(a0)
    mstate=np.zeros(m); vstate=np.zeros(m); tstate=0
    b1=0.9; b2=0.999; eps=1e-8
    best=a0.copy(); best_sc=_score(best)
    current_sc=best_sc
    no_improve=0; no_improve_current=0
    lr=lr_start
    capv=cap
    it=0
    span=max(1e-6, t_end-t_start)
    while time.monotonic() < t_end - 0.2:
       it+=1
       frac=min(1.0,max(0.0,(time.monotonic()-t_start)/span))
       alpha=alpha_start + (alpha_end-alpha_start)*frac
       focus=focus_start + (focus_end-focus_start)*frac
       lr=lr_start + (lr_end-lr_start)*frac
       y=_center_clip(y)
       a=softmax_y(y)
       c=np.convolve(a,a)
       cm=float(c.max())
       sc=2.0*m*cm
       # if nonfinite break
       if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
           best_sc=sc; best=a.copy(); no_improve=0
           # small lr increase
           lr = min(lr*1.03 + 1e-4, 0.35)
           current_sc=sc; no_improve_current=0
       else:
           no_improve +=1
           if sc > best_sc + 0.05 and it>100:
               # reset to best
               y=_log_from_a(best)+0.1*rng.standard_normal(m); mstate=...; tstate=0; no_improve=0; continue
           no_improve_current +=1
       # weights
       w=_make_weights(c, focus, alpha)
       ga=_grad_weights(a,w)
       gz=a*(ga - np.dot(ga,a))
       # clean
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(gz)):
          mz? reset
       tstate +=1
       mstate = b1*mstate + (1-b1)*gz
       vstate = b2*vstate + (1-b2)*gz*gz
       mb1=1-b1**tstate; mb2=1-b2**tstate
       step=lr * (mstate/mb1)/(np.sqrt(vstate/mb2)+eps)
       step=np.clip(step, -cap, cap)
       # optional line search: try step, half
       y_trial=y-step
       # evaluate maybe 1 step only? Let's do simple: accept if finite and sc_trial <= sc + 1e-10; else try 0.5 then 0.25.
       accept_alpha=0.0
       for al in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125):
           y_tr=y - step*al
           a_tr=_softmax_y(y_tr)
           sc_tr=_score(a_tr)
           if np.isfinite(sc_tr) and sc_tr <= sc + 1e-9:
               accept_alpha=al; break
           # if al==0.125 and sc_tr < best_sc? update? We'll update after.
       if accept_alpha>0:
           y = y - step*accept_alpha
           # If line search accepted, best already updated if sc<=best; else current score is sc_tr.
           # We don't have sc_tr variable if accepted; maybe evaluate again or store.
           a=_softmax_y(y); c=...; sc=...
           if sc < best_sc: update
       else:
           # accept tiny exploratory step with probability 0.3 if finite maybe step*0.05
           if rng.random()<0.25:
               y = y - step*0.05
           # else no move, increase no_improve
           no_improve +=1
       # center/clip
       y=_center_clip(y)
       # restart logic
       if no_improve > 800 or (no_improve > 200 and sc > best_sc + 0.02):
           noise = 0.05 + 0.05*min(1,it/1000)
           y = _log_from_a(best) + noise*rng.standard_normal(m)
           mstate=np.zeros(m); vstate=np.zeros(m); tstate=0
           no_improve=0
       if it % 400 == 0:
           a_pol, sc_pol = _quick_polish(best, rng, max_evals=50, time_limit_ratio=...)
           if sc_pol < best_sc: best=a_pol; best_sc=sc_pol; y=_log_from_a(best)+0.03*rng.standard_normal(m); state reset
    return best,best_sc
```

Line search calling `_score` 4 times per iter doubles cost. We can simplify: accept if `sc_trial <= sc + 1e-8` for alpha=1, else try half. Max 3 evals. For n=127 okay. But 10k iter *3 conv =48M small ops? actually high but okay maybe 30s. We can reduce by no line search and use current score from y after step? Need score anyway for next iter. We can compute next iter's score only, accept step if finite. The line search avoids big bad steps. Let's do at most 2 evals: full and half. If full not better (sc_trial > sc + 1e-8), try half. If half not, accept 0.25 untested? Maybe no. To save time, no line search: just `y = y - step`; after next loop compute score. But if step bad, optimizer may wander far. Cap small. Previous no line search okay. Use no line search for speed, with reset if sc > best + 0.02. Let's start no line search, simpler and faster. We can still evaluate current score each iter. If step leads to much worse, reset after threshold.

Add adaptive lr: if no_improve_current > 50 (current score not decreasing), reduce cap/lr: cap *=0.9; lr=max(0.03,lr*0.9). If best improves, increase cap toward initial.

Could use `sc >= scalar`? Use best_sc.

# next iteration will compute score; but for reset we need current_sc. We can compute after step maybe using cheap? We'll compute at top next loop. So keep current_sc from before update? For reset within same loop, not.

Local Polish `_quick_polish`:
Inputs best, rng, max_evals maybe 50. Use current time called intra optimizer; need check time. It may update best. Implementation efficient:
```
def _pair_polish(a, rng, max_evals=80):
   best=a.copy(); sc=_score(best); n=best.size; evals=0; tcall=time.monotonic()
   while evals < max_evals and time.monotonic()-tcall < 2.0:
      # choose random i,j
      i=rng.integers(0,n); j=rng.integers(0,n)
      if i==j: continue
      # local line search around ratio? sample 7 deltas
      base = max(1e-8, min(best[i], best[j])*0.1)
      for d in [0.5*base, -0.5*base, 0.2*base, -0.2*base, base, -base]:
         b=best.copy(); b[i]+=d; b[j]-=d
         if b[i]<0 or b[j]<0: continue
         s=b.sum(); if s<=0: continue
         b/=s
         scb=_score(b)
         evals+=1
         if scb < sc - 1e-12:
             best=b; sc=scb; # restart deltas around new
             break
      # block scaling
      if rng.random()<0.3:
         L=int(rng.integers(2, max(3,n//4)))
         s0=int(rng.integers(0,n-L+1))
         for f in [0.8,0.9,1.1,1.3,0.7]:
             b=best.copy(); b[s0:s0+L]*=f; b/=b.sum(); scb=_score(b); evals+=1; if improve update break
   return best,sc
```
This can find small improvements.

But in optimizer, calling polish every 400 iters with 80 evals may dominate (400*16k=6.4M conv ops vs polish 80*16k=1.3M) okay.

```
def _a_from_d(d):
   y=np.empty(m); y[:m-1]=d; y[m-1]=0
   # For stability, subtract max before exp (softmax invariant)
   z=y-y.max()
   e=np.exp(z); a=e/e.sum(); return a
```

Implementation details for L-BFGS:
```
def _lbfgs_refine(a, beta, maxiter=150, maxfun=300):
   try: from scipy.optimize import minimize
   except: return a, _score(a)
   m=a.size
   d0=np.zeros(m-1) # since a=softmax(d0, last=0) not matching a. Need initialize to log a with last set 0:
   y0=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300)); y0 -= y0[-1]? To set last zero without changing a: y_full = y0 - y0[-1]; d0=y_full[:m-1]. Then softmax([d0,0]) = a (unnormalized identical) if no centering. Good.
   def fun_grad(d):
       y=np.empty(m); y[:m-1]=d; y[-1]=0
       z=y-y.max(); e=np.exp(z); a=e/e.sum()
       c=np.convolve(a,a); cm=c.max()
       zc=beta*(c-cm); zc=np.clip(zc,-60,0); w=np.exp(zc); w /= w.sum()
       phi=2.0*m*(cm + np.log(np.sum(np.exp(zc)))/beta) # logsumexp
       # if beta large and sum exp zero? handle
       ga=_grad_weights(a, 2.0*m*w)  # dPhi/da
       gz=a*(ga - np.dot(ga,a))
       return phi, gz[:m-1]
   res=minimize(fun_grad, d0, jac=True, method='L-BFGS-B', options={'maxiter':maxiter,'maxfun':maxfun,'ftol':1e-12,'gtol':1e-8,'disp':False})
   if res.fun finite: convert d to a, score, if < current return.
```
But SciPy minimize expects first return value scalar, second grad. With jac=True, fun returns (f,g). Good. However L-BFGS-B handles unbounded. It may take many evaluations; maxfun 300. Need clip d? d0 moderate. If d grows, exp overflow? We subtract max in a. Gradient formula with softmax valid even if y large after subtract max? Yes derivative of softmax(y) w.r.t y is a*(delta-a) regardless, but we evaluated a from z=y-max; derivative of softmax(z) w.r.t y? Since z_i = y_i - max(y), derivative is not simply delta-a when max changes, but softmax(y) derivative is still a_i(delta_ij - a_i). Because softmax is equivariant and differentiable; derivative formula holds. Good.

But if y contains huge values, y-y.max ok; a fine. Gradient gz = a*(ga - dot) corresponds to derivative wrt y. Good.

Potential issue: We fixed last coordinate to zero, but d variables unconstrained. The mapping d->y_full=[d,0]; adding constant to d not same? It changes relative values; okay. The point d0 has last zero.

Need ensure if res fails but res.x finite, still evaluate.

Now, possible improvement: Use exact active set weights top K uniform instead of softmax dynamic. We can blend: `w = 0.7*maxw + 0.3*top_uniform`. Top uniform over K with K = max(1, min(5, np.sum(c >= cmax - max(1e-7, 0.02*(cmax - c.min())))))`. This may be more stable. Let's implement `_make_weights` with top uniform part:
```
def _make_weights(c, focus, flat_alpha):
  cm=c.max(); idx=np.argsort(c)[::-1]
  top_gap=max(cm - (c[idx[1]] if len(idx)>1 else cm), 1e-12)
  beta=focus/top_gap
  beta=min(max(beta,1.0),1e6)
  z=clip(beta*(c-cm), -60,0); wmax=exp(z); normalize
  # top uniform:
  # choose K: number of top values within tol = max(1e-8, 0.05*top_gap? no)
  tol = max(1e-12, 0.25*top_gap)
  K = int(np.sum(c[idx[:5]] >= cm - tol))
  K = max(1,min(5,K))
  wtop=np.zeros_like(c); wtop[idx[:K]]=1.0/K
  w = (1 - flat_alpha)* (0.6*wmax + 0.4*wtop) + flat_alpha*excess_flat
```
This ensures if top gap tiny, K multiple. If gap large, K=1. Flat_alpha early 0.5. Excess flat:
```
avg=1.0/(2*m-1)
ex=np.maximum(c-avg,0); es=ex.sum(); wflat=ex/es if es>0 else wtop
```
Maybe use wflat=sqrt(ex)/sum? emphasizes more. Use ex^2? Let's use `ex ** 1.5` to emphasize high. But not critical.

We can make flat_alpha parameter `alpha` as fraction. If alpha=0.5, half gradient from excess flat. Good.

Let's set in main: alpha_start=0.6, alpha_end=0.05; focus_start=1.0, focus_end=8.0. Then early: 60% excess flat, 40% (60% max soft 40% top uniform). Late: mostly max/top.

Could top uniform over K=1 at late essentially exact max subgradient. Good.

Now, local polish maybe should use current best and pair transfers. Let's implement a more effective 1D optimization for pair transfer.

For pair (i,j), define f(δ) score with a_i+δ, a_j-δ (renormalized? If we renormalize, sum remains 1 automatically but scaling changes all values. Better keep sum constant without renormalization by δ: b[i]=a[i]+δ, b[j]=a[j]-δ, sum still1. No need normalize. This is a simple line on simplex. We can sample δ in feasible interval [ -a_i, a_j ]. We can choose δ relative to min(a_i,a_j). To allow moving significant mass, sample fracs of min. Use random sign. For each candidate, compute score. We can do a small ternary/golden? The 1D function is max of quadratics in δ, piecewise; sampling 11 points enough. We can evaluate 11 candidates per pair. For 100 pairs =1100 conv, okay. We'll set max_evals maybe 400.

Pair polish algorithm:
```
def _local_polish(a, rng, max_evals=400, time_limit=10):
   best=a.copy(); sc=_score(best); n=best.size; evals=0; t0=time.monotonic()
   while evals < max_evals and time < t0+time_limit:
      i=rng.integers(0,n); j=rng.integers(0,n)
      while i==j: j=...
      amin=min(best[i],best[j])
      # choose a set of fractional deltas relative to amin

```

Could optimize `_grad_weights` for m<=300 using matrix multiplication to avoid Python loop:
We need h[j]=sum_{l=0}^{m-1} a[l] w[l+j]. This is a cross-correlation. We can compute by forming a Toeplitz? For m=127, loop fine. Another vectorized: `h = np.einsum('i,ij->j', a, W)` where W rows = w[j:j+m]. Construct W using stride tricks:
```
s=np.lib.stride_tricks.sliding_window_view(w, m)  # shape (m, m), W[j,l]=w[j+l]
h=s @ a
```
This uses BLAS matrix-vector multiply O(m^2) in C, no Python loop. `sliding_window_view` available numpy >=1.20 likely. Memory m^2=16k, fine. Let's use this for speed:
```
def _grad_weights(a,w):
   m=a.size
   if m <= 0: return ...
   if m <= 512:
      # w length 2m-1
      windows = np.lib.stride_tricks.sliding_window_view(w, m)
      # windows shape (m, m); h[j,l]=w[j+l]
      h = windows @ a
      return 2*h
   else: FFT
```
Check: For m=3,w length5,w[3]=1: windows rows: j=0 [w0,w1,w2]; j=1 [w1,w2,w3]; j=2 [w2,w3,w4]. @a gives h. Good. This is much faster and simpler. Memory for m=512: 512x512=262k floats=2MB, fine. For m=1000: 8MB windows (view no copy) BLAS matvec maybe 1M ops, okay. We can use sliding_window_view for all m <= 2000. For safety, if numpy version lacks sliding_window_view? Use as_strided fallback? Most modern. We can implement fallback:
```
try: slices = np.lib.stride_tricks.sliding_window_view(w,m)
except AttributeError: use np.as_strided(w, (m,m), (w.strides[0], w.strides[0]))
```
But `sliding_window_view` returns shape (len(w)-m+1, m). Since len(w)=2m-1, rows m. Good. Use `np.as_strided` careful not to modify.

This changes gradient computation to BLAS, very fast. Great.

Convolution `np.convolve` is C O(m^2). For m=2000, 4M ops, per iter maybe 2ms; 1200s ~600k iters maybe too many but okay. We restrict.

`_upsample(a,n)`:
```
if a.size==n: return a.copy()
x0=np.linspace(0,1,a.size); x1=np.linspace(0,1,n)
out=np.interp(x1,x0,a)
out=np.maximum(out,0)
s=out.sum()
if s<1e-12: out=np.ones(n)/n
else out/=s
return out
```
If downsample, same.

`_make_inits(n,rng,parent=None,max_inits=20)`:
```
inits=[]; def add(arr): arr=np.asarray(arr,float); arr=np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0, posinf=1e6, neginf=0); arr=np.clip(arr,0,1e6); s=arr.sum(); if s>1e-9: arr=arr/s; if finite add
x=np.arange(n,float)
if parent: add(_upsample(parent,n)); add(_upsample(parent,n)*np.exp(0.1*normal)); ...
add(np.ones(n))
if n>1:
  for p in [-1.0,-0.5,-0.2,0.0,0.2,0.5,1.0,1.5,2.0]: add((x+1)**p) # p negative ok
  for p in [-0.5,0,0.5,1.0]:
    for q in [-0.5,0,0.5,1.0]:
       add((x+1)**p * (n-x)**q)  # for p/negative at x=n? use n-x+1?
  for g in [-3,-1.5,-0.5,0.5,1.5,3]: add(np.exp(g*x/(max(1,n-1))))
  for f in [1,2,3]: add(1+0.1*np.sin(2*np.pi*f*x/(n-1))+1e-4)
  add(_softmax_normal(rng.normal(scale=0.5,size=n)))
  add(_softmax_normal(rng.normal(scale=1.5,size=n)))
  # two-hump
  add(1 + 0.3*np.exp(-((x-n*0.2)/ (n*0.1))**2) + 0.3*np.exp(-((x-n*0.8)/(n*0.1))**2))
```
Limit maybe 24 to avoid evaluation overhead (small).

For p negative and x=0, (x+1)^p finite; for q with (n-x) at x=n-1 is1. Good.

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    t_start=time.monotonic()
    hard = t_start + max(10.0, float(budget_s) - 15.0)
    # parent and best
    parent=None; n_main=127
    try: ...
    if parent is not None: n_main=parent.size
    else: parent=np.ones(n_main)/n_main
    else normalize
    best=parent.copy(); best_sc=_score(best)
    # quick inits same length
    init_list=_make_inits(n_main,rng,parent=parent,max_inits=18)
    for a in init_list: sc=_score(a); if sc<best_sc: update
    # decide hetero budget
    remaining=hard-t_start
    hetero_budget = 0.0
    if remaining > 300:
       hetero_budget = min(90.0, 0.12*remaining)
    main_end = hard - hetero_budget - 2.0
    # if main_end too close, set hard-2
    # Main stage 1
    end1 = t_start + 0.55*(main_end-t_start)
    best,best_sc=_run_optimizer(best, t_start+0.01, end1, rng, lr_start=0.16, lr_end=0.10, alpha_start=0.65, alpha_end=0.25, focus_start=1.0, focus_end=6.0, cap=0.35, polish_every=700)
    # Stage 2
    end2 = main_end
    best,best_sc=_run_optimizer(best, time.monotonic(), end2, rng, lr_start=0.10, lr_end=0.05, alpha_start=0.15, alpha_end=0.0, focus_start=4.0, focus_end=12.0, cap=0.22, polish_every=600)
    # L-BFGS stages if time
    if time.monotonic() < main_end - 5:
       for beta in (300, 5000, 30000):
          bsc, bsc? = _lbfgs_refine(best, beta, maxiter=80, maxfun=200)
          update
       # local polish
    best,best_sc=_quick_polish(best,rng,max_evals=300,time_limit=20)
    # hetero lengths
    hetero_start=time.monotonic()
    if hetero_budget > 10 and time.monotonic() < hard - 15:
       lengths=[]
       # include neighbors and a few longer
       candidates_lengths = [n_main-2,n_main-1,n_main+1,n_main+2, int(n_main*1.1), int(n_main*1.25), int(n_main*1.5)]
       for L in ... valid 2..512 not n_main, add
       per_time = max(6.0, (hard - hetero_start - 12)/max(1,len(lengths)))
       for L in lengths:
          if time >= hard-12: break
          a0=_upsample(best,L)
          # also quick inits L maybe 4
          for a in _make_inits(L,rng,parent=best,max_inits=5): evaluate update
          endL=min(hard-12, time.monotonic()+per_time)
          if endL > time+3:
             bestc,sc=_run_optimizer(a0, time.monotonic(), endL, rng, ... cap adapted)
             if sc<best_sc: update
             bestc,sc=_quick_polish(...)
    # final validate
    cands=[]; add best; maybe short fallback
    final_list=None; final_score=inf
    for cand in cands: lst...; sc=evaluate_sequence(lst); update
    if final_list is None: fallback [1.0]
    return final_list
```

Let's detail `_run_optimizer` with polish:

```
def _run_optimizer(a0, t_start, t_end, rng, ..., polish_every=0, polish_evals=60, polish_time=3):
    if t_end <= time.monotonic() + 0.5: return a0,_score(a0)
    a0=_normalize(a0)
    best=a0.copy(); best_sc=_score(best)
    y=_log_from_a(a0)
    m=best.size
    state...
    it=0; no_improve=0; capv=cap; lr=lr_start
    span=max(1e-6,t_end-t_start); last_polish=t_start
    while time.monotonic() < t_end - 0.3:
       it+=1
       frac=min(1,max(0,(time.monotonic()-t_start)/span))
       alpha=...
       focus=...
       lr_cur=lr_start + (lr_end-lr_start)*frac
       y=_center_clip(y)
       a=_soft_from_y(y)
       c=_conv(a)
       cm=c.max(); sc=2*m*cm
       if not finite: break
       if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
           best_sc=sc; best=a.copy(); no_improve=0
           capv=min(0.45, capv*1.02+0.001)
       else:
           no_improve += 1
           if no_improve > 200:
              capv=max(0.05, capv*0.98)
       w=_make_weights(c, focus, alpha)
       ga=_grad_weights(a,w)
       gz=a*(ga - np.dot(ga,a))
       if not finite: reset state; continue
       # Adam update
       tstate+=1; ...
       step=lr_cur*(mstate/mb1)/(np.sqrt(vstate/mb2)+1e-8)
       step=np.clip(step, -capv, capv)
       y = y - step
       y=_center_clip(y)
       # periodic polish
       if polish_every and it % polish_every == 0 and t_end - time.monotonic() > polynomial:
           pol, polsc = _quick_polish(best, rng, max_evals=polish_evals, time_limit=polish_time)
           if polsc < best_sc - 1e-12:
               best_sc=polsc; best=pol.copy()
               y=_log_from_a(best) + 0.02*rng.standard_normal(m)
               state reset; no_improve=0
       # reset if stuck
       if no_improve > max(500, 300*m/127?) or (no_improve > 150 and sc > best_sc + 0.03):
           noise=0.05+0.05*min(1.0, it/2000)
           y=_log_from_a(best) + noise*rng.standard_normal(m)
           state reset; no_improve=0
           lr_cur? (schedule handles)
    # final local small?
    return best,best_sc
```

Potential inefficiency: If no improve for 500, but current sc maybe slightly worse; reset. Good.

No line search: If step too large, sc may blow; reset when sc > best+0.03 after 150 no improve. Could be thrown away. Capv reduce.

Should we evaluate score after step for line search? We evaluate at top next iter. If step leads to NaN, next loop score nonfinite and break? We can check y finite after step; if not reset.

`_center_clip`:
```
def _center_clip(y, limit=25.0):
   y=np.nan_to_num(y, nan=0, posinf=limit, neginf=-limit)
   y = y - y.mean()
   return np.clip(y, -limit, limit)
```
Limit 25 ratio e^50=5e21. Enough. Initial log parent values maybe >30? If some value extremely small relative to max, clipped, but okay.

`_soft_from_y`:
```
z=y - y.max()
z=np.clip(z, -700, 0) # but y limited so -50
e=np.exp(z); s=e.sum(); if s<=0: uniform; return e/s
```
Since y centered clipped [-25,25], z range [-50,0]. exp(-50) 1.9e-22. Good.

Adam bias variables:
```
mb1=0.0; mb2=0.0; tstate=0
def reset(): mstate.fill(0); vstate.fill(0); tstate=0; mb1=0; mb2=0
```
Inside:
```
tstate +=1; mb1 = (1-b1)+b1*mb1; mb2=(1-b2)+b2*mb2
mstate = b1*mstate + (1-b1)*gz
vstate = b2*vstate + (1-b2)*gz*gz
step = lr_cur * (mstate/mb1) / (np.sqrt(vstate/mb2)+1e-8)
```

If gz has huge values? We can sanitize: `gz = np.nan_to_num(gz, nan=0, posinf=1e6, neginf=-1e6)`; maybe scale? Adam normalizes per coord, but if some coord huge and others tiny, step for huge coord lr, others step small? Actually Adam per coord step ~lr if gradient direction stable, regardless magnitude. If a coord gradient NaN, reset. Good.

But if gz all zero, step zero. If objective flat, no improve; reset eventually.

Now, local polish implementation with sliding vectorized? scoring many candidates. We'll write simple copy and conv. For pair line, we can avoid duplicating full array for each delta by computing b=best.copy() inside loop. okay.

`_quick_polish` parameters: `max_evals=100, time_limit=5`. It should not use `_score` too many. Use `evals` count.

Pseudo:
```
def _quick_polish(a, rng, max_evals=100, time_limit=5.0):
    best=_normalize(a); sc=_score(best); n=best.size
    if n==1: return best,sc
    t0=time.monotonic(); evals=0
    fracs_pair=[0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.6,1.0]
    factors=[0.7,0.85,1.15,1.4]
    while evals < max_evals and time.monotonic() - t0 < time_limit:
       # pair
       i=int(rng.integers(0,n)); j=int(rng.integers(0,n))
       if i==j: continue
       base=min(best[i],best[j])
       if base <= 1e-12: base=max(best[i],best[j])*0.1
       improved=False
       for sgn in (1,-1):
          for fr in fracs_pair:
             d=sgn*fr*base
             if -best[i]-1e-12 < d < best[j]+1e-12:
                b=best.copy(); b[i]+=d; b[j]-=d
                if b[i]<0 or b[j]<0: continue
                # sum remains 1 (except tiny), normalize just in case
                s=b.sum();
                if s<=0: continue
                if abs(s-1)>1e-12: b/=s
                scb=_score(b); evals+=1
                if scb < sc:
                   best=b; sc=scb; improved=True; base=min(best[i],best[j])
                   break
          if improved: break
       # block
       if rng.random()<0.4:
          L=int(rng.integers(2, max(3, min(n, n//5+1))))
          s0=int(rng.integers(0, max(1,n-L+1)))
          for f in factors:
             b=best.copy(); b[s0:s0+L]*=f
             s=b.sum(); if s>0: b/=s
             scb=_score(b); evals+=1
             if scb < sc:
                best=b; sc=scb; factors... break
    return best,sc
```
Feasible condition should be `d >= -best[i]` and `d <= best[j]`. If d positive adds to i, subtracts j; if d negative opposite. So condition `d > -best[i] - 1e-12 and d < best[j] + 1e-12`.

Use `base` maybe too small if best[i] or best[j] tiny. Could use `base = max(0.02*min(best[i],best[j]), 1e-4)`? But fractions cover 1.0 of min, so max move half of smaller. Could be limited. Also try bigger based on difference:
```
base = max(min(best[i],best[j]), 0.05*(best[i]-best[j]) if best[i]>best[j] else ...)*0.2
```
Simpler: choose d from `rng.uniform(-0.2,0.2)*(best[i]+best[j])` and clip feasible. For line search, do 7 random d. This may be better.
```
span = best[i]+best[j]
for _ in range(7):
   d = rng.uniform(-0.5,0.5)*span
   d = max(-best[i]*0.95, min(best[j]*0.95, d))
```
This samples enough. Use after improvement, restart.

Block scaling factors can be random too.

Local polish evals maybe 300 at end, time 20s. Good.

Now, L-BFGS refine returning best:
```
def _lbfgs_refine(a, beta, maxiter=100, maxfun=250):
    try: from scipy.optimize import minimize
    except Exception: return _normalize(a), _score(a)
    m=a.size
    if m<2: return...
    a0=_normalize(a)
    y0=np.log(np.maximum(a0,1e-300))
    y0 = y0 - y0[-1]
    d0=y0[:m-1]
    def fun_grad(d):
       y=np.empty(m); y[:m-1]=d; y[-1]=0
       # stability: y finite? 
       z=y-y.max()
       e=np.exp(np.clip(z,-700,0)); s=e.sum()
       if s<=0: return np.inf, np.zeros(m-1)
       aa=e/s
       c=np.convolve(aa,aa); cm=float(c.max())
       if not finite: return np.inf, ...
       zc=beta*(c-cm); zc=np.clip(zc,-60,0); e2=np.exp(zc); s2=e2.sum()
       if s2<=0: w=np.zeros_like(c); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1
       else: w=e2/s2
       logsum=cm + np.log(s2)/beta
       phi=2.0*m*logsum
       ga=_grad_weights(aa, 2.0*m*w)
       gz=aa*(ga - np.dot(ga,aa))
       grad=np.nan_to_num(gz[:m-1], nan=0, posinf=0, neginf=0)
       return float(phi), grad
    res=minimize(fun_grad, d0, jac=True, method='L-BFGS-B', options={'maxiter':maxiter,'maxfun':maxfun,'ftol':1e-12,'gtol':1e-8,'disp':False})
    d=res.x if hasattr(res,'x') and np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)) else d0
    y=...; aa=...
    sc=_score(aa)
    if finite and sc < _score(a0): return aa,sc
    return a0, ...
```
But `res.fun` may not be best if stopped; res.x is iterate. Good.
Potential issue: minimize's `maxfun` counts function calls; 250 okay.
Timeout not controlled; if n=512 and maxiter100, line search might be slow. We call only main length 127, maxfun200. Fine.

Could L-BFGS gradient scale huge (phi ~1.5, grad maybe 0.01) good.

Now, in `propose_candidate`, after main Adam, L-BFGS with beta maybe 200 (smooth), 5000, 50000. But if beta 50000 LSE almost max, gradient concentrated; L-BFGS may do small steps. Run each maybe 5s.
We need check `time.monotonic()` before/after; if too long, skip.

Now, should we use `_make_weights` with focus as `beta/top_gap`; if `top_gap` very small, beta huge, z for top tensor maybe 0 and lower -60. Good. But `focus` could be 12. Top2 e^-12 ~6e-6, almost exact. Good.

Could adding `flat_alpha` positive excess be enough? Let's write `_make_weights` elegantly:
```
def _make_weights(c, focus, flat_alpha):
    cm=float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(cm): return uniform? 
    # top gap using partition
    if c.size <=1:
       return np.ones_like(c)/c.size
    # get top two efficiently
    # argsort small
    idx=np.argsort(c)[::-1]
    gap=cm - float(c[idx[1]])
    gap=max(gap, 1e-12*max(1.0, cm))
    beta=focus/gap
    beta=float(np.clip(beta, 1.0, 1.0e6))
    z=np.clip(beta*(c-cm), -60.0, 0.0)
    e=np.exp(z); s=e.sum()
    if s <=0:
       wmax=np.zeros_like(c); wmax[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0
    else:
       wmax=e/s
    # top uniform within tol
    tol=max(1e-12*max(1.0,cm), 0.25*gap)
    K = int(np.sum(c[idx[:5]] >= cm - tol))
    K = max(1, min(5, K))
    wtop=np.zeros_like(c); wtop[idx[:K]] = 1.0/K
    wcore = 0.6*wmax + 0.4*wtop
    if flat_alpha > 1e-6:
       avg=1.0/float(c.size)
       ex=np.maximum(c-avg, 0.0)
       es=float(ex.sum())
       if es > 1e-15:
          wflat = ex/es
          # maybe sharpen: wflat = (ex / np.sqrt(es)) ??? no
       else:
          wflat = wtop
       w = (1.0-flat_alpha)*wcore + flat_alpha*wflat
    else:
       w = wcore
    s=w.sum()
    if s <=0: w=wtop
    else: w/=s
    return w
```

If g is optimal extreme, it may be a complementary sequence. Rescale to sum1, blend. This might jump to better sequence. We could use this as occasional "dual step" instead of gradient.

Constraints for LP: For each k, sum_i f[i]*g[k-i] <= M. Variables g length n. Matrix A shape (2n-1, n): A[k,j]=f[k-j] if 0<=k-j<n else 0. For n=127, 253x127 dense. Maximize sum g (c = -ones for minimization) s.t. A g <= M, g>=0. Use scipy.optimize.linprog method='highs'. Solve maybe 0.01-0.1s. Then g0. Rescale to sum1, blend f <- (1-t)f + t g. This could be a strong move. The LP feasible g=0; optimum positive. Since f sum1, M~0.006, A entries ~0.008, constraints allow g up to ~0.7. Fine.

Why this helps? It finds a sequence g whose convolution with f is bounded by current peak, maximizing mass; then blending may reduce peak due to dual ascent. The previous description used it. We can incorporate as occasional dual step when stuck.

LP details:
```
def _dual_step(f, M, time_limit):
   n=f.size
   A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n))
   for j in range(n):
       # for k from j to j+n-1, A[k,j]=f[k-j]
       A[j:j+n-1, j] = f  # Because k=j+l => f[l]
   # Wait A[k,j] = f[k-j] if 0<=k-j<n else 0. For each j, k ranges j..j+n-1, values f[0..n-1]. Yes.
   b=np.full(2*n-1, M)
   c=-np.ones(n)
   res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=[(0,None)]*n, method='highs')
   if res.success: g=res.x; if sum>0: g/=sum
   return g
```
Check constraint: (f*g)[k] = sum_i f[i] g[k-i]. Let variable index j for g_j. f*g = f * g (convolution). Coefficient of g_j in (f*g)[k] is f[k-j] (since convolution sum over i f[i] g[k-i], set i=k-j). Yes. For k from j to j+n-1, A[k,j]=f[k-j]=f[0..n-1]. Good.

Implement `_dual_blend(f, rng, max_blends=6)`:
```
def _dual_blend(f, rng, time_budget=2.0):
   try: from scipy.optimize import linprog
   except: return f,_score(f)
   f=_normalize(f); m=f.size; M=float(np.max(np.convolve(f,f)))
   # Maybe use M * 1.001 to allow slight violation.
   A=np.zeros((2*m-1,m),float)
   for j in range(m): A[j:j+m-1, j] = f  # copy
   c=-np.ones(m)
   b=np.full(2*m-1, M*1.001)
   res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*m, method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
   if res is None or not res.success: return f,...
   g=res.x
   g=np.maximum(g,0); s=g.sum()
   if s < 1e-9: return f
   g/=s
   best=f; best_sc=_score(f)
   for t in [0.05,0.1,0.15,0.25,0.4,0.6]:
      h=(1-t)*f + t*g; h=_normalize(h); sc=_score(h); if sc<best_sc update
   return best,best_sc
```

Detailed code draft:

```
import numpy as np
import time

def _normalize(a):
    a=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size==0: return np.ones(1)
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)/float(a.size)
    return a/s

def _score(a):
    a=_normalize(a)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.inf
    c=np.convolve(a,a)
    if c.size==0: return np.inf
    mx=float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mx): return np.inf
    return float(2.0*n*mx) # since sum normalized to 1
```

`_score` normalizes each call, which for optimizer can be overhead but small. It changes `a` if tiny negatives. Good.

`_grad_weights`:
```
def _grad_weights(a,w):
    m=a.size
    if m==0: return np.zeros(0)
    # ensure w length 2m-1
    if w.size != 2*m-1:
       # pad/trim
    if m <= 2048:
       try:
           windows = np.lib.stride_tricks.sliding_window_view(w, m)
           h = windows @ a
       except Exception:
           h = np.empty(m)
           for j in range(m): h[j]=np.dot(a, w[j:j+m])
       return 2.0*h
    else:
       N=1
       need=3*m-1
       while N<need: N*=2
       A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
       W=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
       h=np.fft.irfft(A*np.conj(W),N)[:m]
       return 2.0*h
```
If using sliding_window_view, w must be contiguous. It is. `windows @ a` does (m,m) @ (m,) returns (m,). Good.

Need if m=1, `sliding_window_view(w,1)` shape (1,1), ok.

```
def _lp_direction(f, M):
   n=f.size
   A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
   for j in range(n): A[j:j+n-1, j] = f
   c=-np.ones(n)
   b=np.full(2*n-1, M*1.0005+1e-12)
   bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
   res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
   if res.success and res.x is not None: return np.maximum(res.x,0)
   return None
```

`_dual_improve(a, max_calls=3)`:
```
def _dual_improve(a, max_calls=3, time_limit=8.0):
   best=_normalize(a); best_sc=_score(best)
   t0=time.monotonic()
   for _ in range(max_calls):
       if time.monotonic() > t0 + time_limit or time.monotonic() > global? no global. Use t_start? Not.
       cur=best
       c=np.convolve(cur,cur); M=float(np.max(c))
       g=_lp_direction(cur, M)
       if g is None: break
       s=g.sum()
       if s <= 1e-9: break
       g/=s
       improved=False
       for t in (0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.6):
           h=(1-t)*cur + t*g
           h=_normalize(h)
           sc=_score(h)
           if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
               best_sc=sc; best=h.copy(); cur=h.copy(); improved=True
       if not improved:
           break
   return best,best_sc
```

```
def _score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size ==0: return np.inf
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-12: return np.inf
    if abs(s-1.0) > 1e-12:
       a = a/s
    n=a.size
    c=np.convolve(a,a)
    if c.size==0: return np.inf
    mx=float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mx): return np.inf
    return float(2.0*n*mx)
```
If a sum not normalized and not 1, this normalizes. For optimizer a sum 1. Good.

`_score_unit` maybe same but with no finite check? Use `_score`.

Potential syntax with `np.lib.stride_tricks.sliding_window_view` may not exist in old numpy. We can fallback.

```
def _grad_weights(a,w):
    m=a.size
    if m ==0: return np.zeros(0)
    if w.size < 2*m-1:
       w=np.pad(w, (0, 2*m-1-w.size))
    elif w.size > 2*m-1:
       w=w[:2*m-1]
    if m <= 4096:
       try:
           view = np.lib.stride_tricks.sliding_window_view(w, m)
           # sliding_window_view shape (m,m); if no? check shape
           if view.shape[0] == m and view.shape[1] == m:
               h = view @ a
               return 2.0*h
       except Exception:
           pass
       h=np.empty(m, dtype=np.float64)
       for j in range(m):
           h[j]=np.dot(a, w[j:j+m])
       return 2.0*h
    else:
       N=1; need=3*m-2
       while N<need: N <<=1
       Ar=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
       Wr=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
       h=np.fft.irfft(Ar*np.conj(Wr),N)[:m]
       return 2.0*h
```
For m=4096, sliding_window_view view 4096x4096 (32MB stride view, matvec may process 16M ops) okay? Memory no copy but BLAS may require? It may multiply view, works but may be slow. We restrict lengths <=768, so m<=768; view 768x768 4.7MB, fine. Use threshold 4096.

Now, `linprog` import inside `_lp_solve`:
```
def _lp_direction(f, M):
    try: from scipy.optimize import linprog
    except Exception: return None
    n=f.size
    if n>600: return None
    A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
    for j in range(n): A[j:j+n-1,j]=f
    c=-np.ones(n)
    b=np.full(2*n-1, M*1.0005)
    # If b very small and f small, no issue.
    res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n, method='highs')
    if res is not None and res.success and hasattr(res,'x'):
       g=np.asarray(res.x,dtype=np.float64)
       if g.size==n and np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
          return np.maximum(g,0.0)
    return None
```
If M=0, b=0, only g=0. But M>0.

Use `method='highs'` available. If high_s not, maybe cvxopt? But scipy's linprog default high_s in recent. If fails, catch? We can try method=None? If method high_s unavailable, older scipy may raise. Use:
```
try: res=linprog(..., method='highs')
except Exception: res=linprog(...)
```
But no need.

```
def _lp_alternate(a, max_iter=30, time_limit=45.0, max_n=600):
   f=_normalize(a); f_sc=_score(f); best=f.copy(); best_sc=f_sc
   if f.size > max_n or time_limit <=0: return best,best_sc
   t0=time.monotonic()
   alphas=(0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5)
   for it in range(max_iter):
      if time.monotonic() > t0 + time_limit or time.monotonic() > hard? no global. Use local.
      c=np.convolve(f,f); M=float(np.max(c))
      g=_lp_direction(f, M)
      if g is None: break
      s=g.sum(); if s<=1e-9: break
      g/=s
      improved=False
      for alpha in alphas:
          h=(1-alpha)*f + alpha*g
          h=_normalize(h)
          sc=_score(h)
          if sc < f_sc - 1e-12:
              f=h.copy(); f_sc=sc; improved=True
              if sc < best_sc - 1e-12: best_sc=sc; best=h.copy()
              break
      if not improved:
          # try once with larger M (1.05) to get different g
          g=_lp_direction(f, M*1.1)
          if g is not None:
             s=g.sum(); if s>1e-9: g/=s
             for alpha in alphas:
                h=(1-alpha)*f+alpha*g; h=_normalize(h); sc=_score(h)
                if sc < f_sc - 1e-12: ...; break
          if not improved: break
   return best,best_sc
```

```
def _lp_direction2(f):
   n=f.size
   A=np.zeros((2*n-1, n+1))
   for j in range(n): A[j:j+n-1, j]=f
   A[:, n] = -1.0
   b=np.zeros(2*n-1)
   Aeq=np.zeros((1,n+1)); Aeq[0,:n]=1.0
   beq=np.array([1.0])
   c=np.zeros(n+1); c[n]=1.0
   res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, A_eq=Aeq, b_eq=beq, bounds=[(0,None)]*n + [(0,None)], method='highs')
   if success and x finite: g=x[:n]; g=np.maximum(g,0); s=g.sum(); if s>0: return g/s, float(x[n])?
```

`_lp_direction_norm`:
```
def _lp_direction_norm(f, use_fallback=True):
    try: from scipy.optimize import linprog
    except: return None
    n=f.size
    if n == 1: return f.copy(), 0.0
    if n > 800: return None
    A=np.empty((2*n-1, n+1), float)
    A[:, :n]=0.0; A[:, n]=0.0
    for j in range(n):
        A[j:j+n-1, j] = f
    A[:, n] = -1.0
    b=np.zeros(2*n-1)
    Aeq=np.zeros((1,n+1)); Aeq[0,:n]=1.0
    beq=np.array([1.0])
    c=np.zeros(n+1); c[n]=1.0
    bounds=[(0.0,None)]*n + [(0.0,None)]
    try:
       res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, A_eq=Aeq, b_eq=beq, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
    except Exception:
       res=None
    if res is not None and res.success and res.x is not None:
       g=np.asarray(res.x[:n], float); g=np.maximum(g,0); s=g.sum()
       if np.all(np.isfinite(g)) and s > 1e-12:
          return g/s, float(res.x[n])
    if use_fallback:
       ... max sum LP ...
    return None
```
Max sum fallback can use constraints A g <= M? Or normalized min? Maybe not. Could use random fallback.

If u point mass1, sliding sums 1, t=1/n. f itself uniform sliding sum max1/n? sum u=1, uniform sliding sum max =n*(1/n)/? f*u at center =1/n. Actually f uniform, u=f gives f*u=conv uniform/uniform max 1/n. So t=1/n. Many u. LP may return basic point mass, g sparse. Blending uniform with point mass may reduce score? Pseudo:
```
def _lp_alternate(a, rng, max_iter=80, time_limit=60.0, max_n=800):
    f=_normalize(a); f_sc=_score(f); best=f.copy(); best_sc=f_sc
    if f.size > max_n or time_limit <=0: return best,best_sc
    t0=time.monotonic(); alphas=(0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.8); stall=0
    for it in range(max_iter):
       if time - t0 > time_limit: break
       g=None
       # main direction
       res=_lp_direction_norm(f)
       if res is not None: g=res[0]
       if g is None:
          # fallback: maybe reverse previous? break
          if rng is not None: g=_lp_direction_norm(_normalize(f*np.exp(0.05*rng.standard_normal(f.size))))
       if g is None: break
       cand=f; cand_sc=f_sc
       for alpha in alphas:
          h=(1-alpha)*f + alpha*g; h=_normalize(h); sc=_score(h)
          if sc < cand_sc - 1e-12: cand=h; cand_sc=sc
       # also maybe blend with reverse of g
       gr=g[::-1]
       for alpha in alphas[:3]:
          h=(1-alpha)*f + alpha*gr; normalize; sc; if better update
       if cand_sc < f_sc - 1e-12:
          f=cand; f_sc=cand_sc; stall=0
          if f_sc < best_sc: best=f.copy(); best_sc=f_sc
       else:
          stall +=1
          if stall >= 3:
             improved_escape=False
             for _e in range(4):
                if time - t0 > time_limit: break
                fp=_normalize(f*np.exp(0.1*rng.standard_normal(f.size)))
                res=_lp_direction_norm(fp)
                if res is None: continue
                g2=res[0]
                for alpha in alphas:
                   h=(1-alpha)*f + alpha*g2
                   sc=_score(h)
                   if sc < f_sc - 1e-12:
                      f=h; f_sc=sc; best=...; improved_escape=True; break
                if improved_escape: break
             if not improved_escape:
                break
    return best,best_sc
```
This may call many LPs. Time limit protects.

Implement:
```
def _lp_blend(a, rng=None):
   res=_lp_direction_norm(a)
   if res is None: return a,_score(a)
   g=res[0]
   best=a; best_sc=_score(a)
   for alpha in (0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5):
      h=(1-alpha)*a + alpha*g; h=_normalize(h); sc=_score(h); update
   return best,best_sc
```

Now, in `_lp_alternate`, if direction g is very similar to f (dot high), blends may not improve. Could use `g_rev = g[::-1]`.

Now, final code. Let's write final answer now in one fence. Need ensure no syntax errors.

Double-check each function:

`_score`:
```
def _score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0: return np.inf
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-12: return np.inf
    if abs(s-1.0) > 1e-12:
       a=a/s
    n=a.size
    c=np.convolve(a,a)
    if c.size ==0: return np.inf
    mx=float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mx): return np.inf
    return float(2.0*n*mx)
```
If a sum 1 but due float abs(s-1) maybe 1e-16, no normalize. Good.

`_normalize` similar but always normalize if sum not 1? Use:
```
def _normalize(a): ... if s<...; return a/s
```

`_grad_weights`: Use `np.lib.stride_tricks.sliding_window_view`. In fallback, if w not contiguous? `w=np.ascontiguousarray(w)`. Do that.

```
def _grad_weights(a,w):
    m=a.size
    if m==0: return np.zeros(0)
    need=2*m-1
    w=np.asarray(w,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if w.size < need:
       w=np.pad(w, (0, need-w.size), mode='constant')
    elif w.size > need:
       w=w[:need]
    w=np.ascontiguousarray(w)
    if m <= 3000:
       try:
          view=np.lib.stride_tricks.sliding_window_view(w, m)
          if view.shape == (m,m):
             h=view @ a
             if np.all(np.isfinite(h)):
                return 2*h
       except Exception:
          pass
       h=np.empty(m,dtype=np.float64)
       for j in range(m):
          h[j]=float(np.dot(a, w[j:j+m]))
       return 2*h
    else:
       N=1
       while N < 3*m: N <<=1
       Ar=np.fft.rfft(a,N); Wr=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
       h=np.fft.irfft(Ar*np.conj(Wr), N)[:m]
       return 2*h
```
If view matvec returns finite. Good. For m large, FFT. Need N>= n + wlen -1 =3m-1. while N < 3*m ok.

`_make_weights`:
```
def _make_weights(c, focus, flat_alpha):
    c=np.asarray(c,dtype=np.float64)
    if c.size ==0: return np.zeros(1)
    if not all finite: return np.full(c.size,1/c.size)
    cm=float(np.max(c))
    if c.size ==1: return np.array([1.0])
    idx=np.argsort(c)[::-1]
    gap=cm - float(c[idx[1]])
    gap=max(gap, 1e-12*max(1.0, abs(cm)))
    beta=float(np.clip(focus/gap, 1.0, 1e6))
    z=np.clip(beta*(c-cm), -60.0, 0.0)
    e=np.exp(z); s=float(np.sum(e))
    if s > 0 and np.isfinite(s): wmax=e/s
    else: wmax=np.zeros_like(c); wmax[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0
    tol=max(1e-12*max(1.0,abs(cm)), 0.2*gap)
    top5=c[idx[:5]]
    K=int(np.sum(top5 >= cm - tol))
    K=max(1,min(5,K))
    wtop=np.zeros_like(c); wtop[idx[:K]]=1.0/float(K)
    wcore=0.6*wmax + 0.4*wtop
    if flat_alpha > 1e-8:
       avg=float(np.sum(c))/float(c.size)
       if avg <=0: avg=1.0/float(c.size)
       ex=np.maximum(c-avg,0.0)
       es=float(np.sum(ex))
       if es > 1e-15:
          wflat=ex/es
       else:
          wflat=wtop
       w=(1.0-flat_alpha)*wcore + flat_alpha*wflat
    else: w=wcore
    s=float(np.sum(w))
    if not finite or s<=0: w=wtop
    else: w/=s
    return w
```
Use `flat_alpha` can be >1? We pass <=1. If >1, (1-alpha) negative. Clamp: `alpha = min(1.0, max(0.0, flat_alpha))`. Do inside.

`_log_from_a`:
```
def _log_from_a(a, limit=25.0):
   a=np.maximum(np.asarray(a,float), 1e-300)
   y=np.log(a)
   y=np.nan_to_num(y, nan=0.0, posinf=limit, neginf=-limit)
   y -= float(np.mean(y))
   return np.clip(y, -limit, limit)
```

`_soft_from_y`:
```
def _soft_from_y(y, limit=25.0):
   y=np.nan_to_num(y, nan=0.0, posinf=limit, neginf=-limit)
   y = y - np.mean(y)
   y = np.clip(y, -limit, limit)
   z = y - np.max(y)
   e=np.exp(np.clip(z, -700.0, 0.0))
   s=float(np.sum(e))
   if not finite or s<=0: return np.full(y.size,1/y.size)
   return e/s
```
Maybe y clipped limit, z min -50.

`_center_clip` same but no softmax.

`_quick_polish`: Use `rng.integers` may return np.int64. Good.
```
def _quick_polish(a, rng, max_evals=100, time_limit=5.0):
   best=_normalize(a); sc=_score(best); n=best.size
   if n <=1 or time_limit <=0: return best,sc
   t0=time.monotonic(); evals=0
   alphas? factors
   while evals < max_evals and time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
      i=int(rng.integers(0,n)); j=int(rng.integers(0,n))
      if i==j:
         i=0; j=n//2
      lo=-0.95*best[i]; hi=0.95*best[j]
      if hi > lo:
         dvals = np.linspace(lo, hi, 7)
         # add random maybe
         for d in dvals:
            if evals >= max_evals: break
            b=best.copy(); b[i]+=d; b[j]-=d
            if b[i] < -1e-12 or b[j] < -1e-12: continue
            b=np.where(b<0,0,b)
            s=b.sum()
            if s <=0: continue
            if abs(s-1)>1e-9: b/=s
            scb=_score(b); evals+=1
            if scb < sc - 1e-12:
                best=b; sc=scb; break
      if rng.random() < 0.35:
         L=int(rng.integers(2, max(3, min(n, n//4+2))))
         if L < 2 or n <= L: L=max(2,n)
         s0=int(rng.integers(0, max(1,n-L+1)))
         for f in (0.75,0.9,1.1,1.3,1.6):
            if evals >= max_evals: break
            b=best.copy(); b[s0:s0+L]*=f
            s=b.sum(); if s<=0: continue
            if abs(s-1)>1e-9: b/=s
            scb=_score(b); evals+=1
            if scb < sc - 1e-12: best=b; sc=scb; break
   return best,sc
```
If best[i]=0, lo=0, hi=0.95 best[j]; moves from j to i, good. If best[j]=0, lo=-0.95 best[i], hi=0; moves from i to j. Good.

`_lp_direction(f, perturb=0.0, rng=None)`:
```
def _lp_direction(f, perturb=0.0, rng=None):
   try: from scipy.optimize import linprog
   except Exception: return None
   n=f.size
   if n <=1: return _normalize(f)
   if n > 800: return None
   ff=np.maximum(f, 1e-12); ff=_normalize(ff)
   A=np.zeros((2*n-1, n), dtype=np.float64)
   for j in range(n): A[j:j+n-1, j] = ff
   cconv=np.convolve(ff,ff)
   M=float(np.max(cconv))
   if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0: return None
   obj=-np.ones(n)
   if perturb > 0 and rng is not None:
       obj = obj - perturb*rng.random(n)
   b=np.full(2*n-1, M*1.000001)
   bounds=[(0.0, None)]*n
   try:
      res=linprog(obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
   except Exception:
      try: res=linprog(obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds)
      except Exception: return None
   if res is not None and res.success and res.x is not None:
      g=np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
      g=np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
      g=np.maximum(g,0.0)
      s=float(np.sum(g))
      if s > 1e-12:
         return g/s
   return None
```
If f has n=1, conv max =f^2, constraint? direction itself. Return f.

`_lp_blend_once(a, rng)`:
```
def _lp_blend_once(a, rng=None):
   f=_normalize(a); best=f.copy(); best_sc=_score(f)
   g=_lp_direction(f, rng=rng, perturb=1e-7 if rng is not None else 0.0)
   if g is None: return best,best_sc
   for alpha in (0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.8):
       h=(1-alpha)*f + alpha*g; h=_normalize(h); sc=_score(h)
       if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
           best_sc=sc; best=h.copy()
   # reverse g
   gr=g[::-1]
   for alpha in (0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35):
       h=(1-alpha)*f + alpha*gr; ...
   # average g with f? maybe g2=0.5g+0.5f
   return best,best_sc
```
Use f in blend, not best? If best improved within same call, use best as base for subsequent? Could sequentially update. Simpler: current=f; current_sc=f_sc; for each direction (g,gr) and alpha, if blend improves current, update current. Then keep best. Implement:
```
cur=f; cur_sc=f_sc; best=f; best_sc=f_sc
for gdir in [g, g[::-1]]:
   for alpha in alphas:
      h=... cur...
      if sc < cur_sc -1e-12: cur=h; cur_sc=sc; if sc<best_sc update
    # after each direction, cur updated
return best,best_sc
```
This may chain within one LP. Good.

`_lp_alternate` can just call `_lp_blend_once` repeatedly, until no improve or time. It solves LP each time. Simpler:
```
def _lp_alternate(a, rng, max_iter=100, time_limit=60.0):
   f=_normalize(a); f_sc=_score(f); best=f.copy(); best_sc=f_sc
   t0=time.monotonic()
   for it in range(max_iter):
      if time.monotonic() > t0 + time_limit: break
      g, gsc = _lp_blend_once(f, rng)
      if gsc < f_sc - 1e-12:
          f=g.copy(); f_sc=gsc
          if f_sc < best_sc: best_sc=f_sc; best=f.copy()
      else:
          # try random perturb
          if rng is not None:
             fp=_normalize(f*np.exp(0.08*rng.standard_normal(f.size)))
             gp,gpsc=_lp_blend_once(fp,rng)
             if gpsc < f_sc -1e-12:
                 f=gp.copy(); f_sc=gpsc; update; continue
          break
   return best,best_sc
```
Note `_lp_blend_once` uses its own LP. Good. If no improve, break after one random. Could try a few random. Add loop 3.

This may call many LPs, each `_lp_blend_once` one LP and ~10 score evals. Good.

Potential inefficiency: `_lp_blend_once` uses current f and if blend improves, updates f; but still only one LP per call. `_lp_alternate` repeats.

Now, `_run_optimizer` LP blend uses `_lp_blend_once` when stuck. It may call LP multiple times.

Now, `_lbfgs_refine` with LSE. Let's implement maybe simpler and optional. If time.

```
def _lbfgs_refine(a, beta=3000, maxiter=80, maxfun=200):
   try: from scipy.optimize import minimize
   except: return _normalize(a),_score(a)
   a0=_normalize(a); m=a0.size
   if m<2: return a0,_score(a0)
   y0=np.log(np.maximum(a0,1e-300)); y0 -= y0[-1]; d0=y0[:m-1]
   def fun_grad(d):
       d=np.asarray(d,float)
       y=np.empty(m); y[:m-1]=d; y[-1]=0
       z=y-np.max(y); e=np.exp(np.clip(z,-700,0)); s=e.sum()
       if s <=0: return np.inf, np.zeros(m-1)
       aa=e/s
       c=np.convolve(aa,aa)
       cm=float(np.max(c))
       if not np.isfinite(cm): return np.inf, zeros
       if beta <=0: w=np.zeros_like(c); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1
       else:
          zc=beta*(c-cm); zc=np.clip(zc,-60,0); e2=np.exp(zc); s2=e2.sum()
          if s2 <=0: w=zeros argmax
          else: w=e2/s2
       logsum = cm + np.log(max(s2,1e-300))/beta if beta>0 else cm
       phi = 2.0*m*logsum
       ga=_grad_weights(aa, 2.0*m*w)
       gz=aa*(ga - float(np.dot(ga,aa)))
       grad=np.nan_to_num(gz[:m-1], nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
       # check finite
       if not np.isfinite(phi): phi=1e100
       return float(phi), grad
   try:
      res=minimize(fun_grad, d0, jac=True, method='L-BFGS-B', options={'maxiter':maxiter,'maxfun':maxfun,'ftol':1e-12,'gtol':1e-8,'disp':False})
   except Exception:
      return a0,_score(a0)
   d=res.x if... else d0
   y=... aa=...
   sc=_score(aa)
   if finite and sc < _score(a0): return aa,sc
   return a0,_score(a0)
```
If beta=0, LSE not; use max subgradient no smooth; L-BFGS not good. Use beta>=10.

This may have scale: phi ~score, grad ~? okay.

Now, `_make_inits`:
```
def _make_inits(n, rng, parent=None, max_inits=18):
   inits=[]
   def add(x):
       try:
          x=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
          x=np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
          x=np.maximum(x, 0.0)
          s=float(x.sum())
          if x.size==n and s > 1e-12 and len(inits) < max_inits:
             inits.append(x/s)
       except: pass
   if parent is not None and parent.size>0:
       p_parent=_upsample(parent,n)
       add(p_parent)
       if rng is not None:
          add(p_parent*np.exp(0.05*rng.standard_normal(n)))
          add(p_parent*np.exp(0.15*rng.standard_normal(n)))
   add(np.ones(n)/float(n))
   if n > 1:
       x=np.arange(n,dtype=np.float64)
       xs=x/(max(1,n-1))
       for p in (-1.0,-0.5,-0.2,0.1,0.3,0.6,1.0,1.6):
          add((x+1.0)**p)
       for p in (-0.5,0.0,0.5,1.0):
          for q in (-0.5,0.0,0.5,1.0):
             add((x+1.0)**p * (float(n)-x)**q)
       for g in (-3.0,-1.5,-0.5,0.5,1.5,3.0):
          add(np.exp(g*xs))
       for f in (1,2,3):
          add(1.0 + 0.15*np.sin(2*np.pi*f*xs) + 1e-6)
       add(1.0 + 0.4*np.exp(-((xs-0.2)/0.1)**2) + 0.4*np.exp(-((xs-0.8)/0.1)**2))
   if rng is not None:
       add(_soft_from_y(rng.standard_normal(n)*0.5))
       add(_soft_from_y(rng.standard_normal(n)*1.2))
   if not inits:
       inits.append(np.ones(n)/float(n))
   return inits
```
For q with `n-x` at x=n-1 gives 1, ok. For p negative, x+1 positive. Good.
`max_inits` not strictly if parent adds exceed; add checks len < max_inits before maybe skip parent? We need ensure parent included. In add, if len >= max_inits, skip; But parent first so included. Fine.

`_upsample`:
```
def _upsample(a,n):
   a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
   if a.size == n: return _normalize(a)
   if a.size ==0: return np.ones(n)/n
   x0=np.linspace(0,1,a.size); x1=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   out=np.interp(x1,x0,a)
   return _normalize(out)
```

`_run_optimizer` full code. Need be careful with variables reset inside loop (Python scoping). Use local nested reset? We can just assign.

```
def _run_optimizer(a0, t_start, t_end, rng, lr_start=0.16, lr_end=0.07, alpha_start=0.6, alpha_end=0.1, focus_start=1.0, focus_end=8.0, cap_start=0.35, cap_end=0.18, polish_every=800, polish_evals=40, polish_time=3.0, max_lp=3):
    if t_end <= time.monotonic() + 0.3: return _normalize(a0), _score(a0)
    m=a0.size
    best=_normalize(a0); best_sc=_score(best)
    y=_log_from_a(best)
    mstate=np.zeros(m); vstate=np.zeros(m); tstate=0; mb1=0.0; mb2=0.0
    b1=0.9; b2=0.999; eps=1e-8
    no_improve=0; it=0; capv=cap_start; lp_calls=0
    span=max(1e-6, t_end - t_start)
    while time.monotonic() < t_end - 0.3:
       it +=1
       frac=min(1.0, max(0.0, (time.monotonic()-t_start)/span))
       alpha=alpha_start + (alpha_end-alpha_start)*frac
       focus=focus_start + (focus_end-focus_start)*frac
       lr=lr_start + (lr_end-lr_start)*frac
       cap=cap_start + (cap_end-cap_start)*frac
       y=_center_clip(y)
       a=_soft_from_y(y)
       c=np.convolve(a,a)
       if c.size ==0: break
       cm=float(np.max(c))
       if not np.isfinite(cm): break
       sc=2.0*m*cm
       if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
           best_sc=sc; best=a.copy(); no_improve=0
           capv=min(0.5, capv*1.01+0.001)
       else:
           no_improve +=1
           if no_improve > 150:
               capv=max(0.05, capv*0.98)
       w=_make_weights(c, focus, alpha)
       ga=_grad_weights(a,w)
       gz=a*(ga - float(np.dot(ga,a)))
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(gz)):
           gz=np.zeros(m)
       normg=float(np.linalg.norm(gz))
       if normg < 1e-14:
           gz = 1e-3 * rng.standard_normal(m)
       # adam
       tstate += 1
       mb1 = (1.0-b1) + b1*mb1
       mb2 = (1.0-b2) + b2*mb2
       mstate = b1*mstate + (1.0-b1)*gz
       vstate = b2*vstate + (1.0-b2)*(gz*gz)
       step = lr * (mstate/mb1) / (np.sqrt(vstate/mb2) + eps)
       step=np.clip(step, -capv, capv)
       y = y - step
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(y)):
           y=_log_from_a(best) + 0.05*rng.standard_normal(m)
           mstate.fill(0); vstate.fill(0); tstate=0; mb1=0; mb2=0
           no_improve=0
           continue
       # periodic polish
       if polish_every > 0 and it % polish_every == 0 and t_end - time.monotonic() > polish_time + 1.0:
           pol, polsc = _quick_polish(best, rng, max_evals=polish_evals, time_limit=polish_time)
           if polsc < best_sc - 1e-12:
               best=pol; best_sc=polsc
               y=_log_from_a(best) + 0.03*rng.standard_normal(m)
               mstate.fill(0); vstate.fill(0); tstate=0; mb1=0; mb2=0; no_improve=0
       # stuck LP or reset
       if no_improve > 600 or (no_improve > 200 and sc > best_sc + 0.02):
           did=False
           if lp_calls < max_lp and t_end - time.monotonic() > 6.0 and m <= 800:
               lp_calls +=1
               pol, polsc = _lp_blend_once(best, rng)
               if polsc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                   best=pol; best_sc=polsc; did=True
               elif polsc < best_sc: # not enough?
                   pass
           if not did:
               if time.monotonic() > t_end - 3.0: break
               noise=0.04 + 0.06*min(1.0, float(it)/5000.0)
               y=_log_from_a(best) + noise*rng.standard_normal(m)
               mstate.fill(0); vstate.fill(0); tstate=0; mb1=0; mb2=0
               no_improve=0
    # final quick polish maybe outside
    return best, best_sc
```
Note: `cap` computed but we use `capv` adaptive. Could just use `capv` initial from cap_start and never update to cap_end. Add `target_cap = cap_start + (cap_end-cap_start)*frac`; when no_improve, adjust capv toward target. Simpler: set `capv = cap` each iteration, but no_improve reduce override. If best improve, maybe reset to min(cap_start, cap). Let's implement:
```
target_cap = cap_start + (cap_end-cap_start)*frac
if no_improve ==0: capv=target_cap
else: capv = max(0.05, min(target_cap, capv*0.98))
```
But in code above, no. We can leave capv initialized cap_start and only decay; okay.

`_lp_blend_once` might take LP time; we check.

In `_lp_blend_once`, if `score` uses `_score` with normalize; good.

Now, `propose_candidate`:

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    t0=time.monotonic()
    tb=max(1.0,float(budget_s))
    headroom=min(25.0, max(5.0, 0.02*tb))
    hard=t0 + tb - headroom

    parent=None; n_main=127
    try:
        p=np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if p.size >0:
            pp=np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
            pp=np.maximum(pp,0.0)
            if float(np.sum(pp)) > 0.01:
                parent=pp/float(np.sum(pp))
                n_main=int(parent.size)
    except Exception:
        parent=None
    if parent is None:
        parent=np.ones(n_main)/float(n_main)
    # safeguard large
    if parent.size > 2048:
       parent=_upsample(parent, 2048); n_main=2048

    best=parent.copy(); best_sc=_score(best)
    # quick inits
    for a in _make_inits(n_main, rng, parent=parent, max_inits=14):
       sc=_score(a)
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
          best=a.copy(); best_sc=sc

    if hard - t0 < 20:
        # just final validate
        ...
    remaining=hard-t0
    hetero_budget = 0.0
    if remaining > 400:
       hetero_budget = min(100.0, 0.10*remaining)
    main_end = hard - hetero_budget - 2.0
    now=t0
    # LP phase
    lp_end=min(main_end, t0 + min(120.0, 0.25*(main_end-t0)))
    if lp_end > now + 10:
       best,best_sc=_lp_alternate(best, rng, max_iter=120, time_limit=max(0.0, lp_end-now))
       now=time.monotonic()
    # Adam stage 1
    end1=min(main_end, now + 0.5*(main_end-now))
    if end1 > now + 5:
       best,best_sc=_run_optimizer(best, now, end1, rng, lr_start=0.16, lr_end=0.09, alpha_start=0.55, alpha_end=0.2, focus_start=1.0, focus_end=6.0, polish_every=900, polish_evals=40, polish_time=3.0, max_lp=2)
       now=time.monotonic()
    # Adam stage 2 to main_end
    if main_end > now + 5:
       best,best_sc=_run_optimizer(best, now, main_end, rng, lr_start=0.09, lr_end=0.04, alpha_start=0.12, alpha_end=0.0, focus_start=5.0, focus_end=12.0, polish_every=800, polish_evals=50, polish_time=4.0, max_lp=4)
       now=time.monotonic()
    # L-BFGS
    if main_end > time.monotonic() + 5 and best.size <= 512:
       for beta in (800, 8000, 80000):
          if time.monotonic() > main_end - 4: break
          b2,b2sc=_lbfgs_refine(best, beta=beta, maxiter=60, maxfun=150)
          if b2sc < best_sc - 1e-12: best=b2; best_sc=b2sc
    # dual/LP blend and polish
    if main_end > time.monotonic() and best.size <= 800:
       b2,b2sc=_lp_blend_once(best, rng)
       if b2sc < best_sc: best=b2; best_sc=b2sc
       b2,b2sc=_lp_blend_once(best, rng)
       if b2sc < best_sc: best=b2; best_sc=b2sc
    best,best_sc=_quick_polish(best, rng, max_evals=400, time_limit=max(5.0, min(30.0, hard-time.monotonic()-0.5)))
    # hetero lengths
    if hetero_budget > 15 and time.monotonic() < hard - 15:
       hetero_end=hard - 8.0
       lengths=[]
       for L in [n_main-2,n_main-1,n_main+1,n_main+2, int(round(n_main*1.15)), int(round(n_main*1.3)), int(round(n_main*1.5))]:
          if L > 1 and L <= 768 and L != n_main:
             lengths.append(int(L))
       lengths=sorted(set(lengths))
       if lengths:
          per_time=max(5.0, min(20.0, (hetero_end - time.monotonic())/len(lengths)))
          for L in lengths:
             if time.monotonic() > hetero_end - 5: break
             a0=_upsample(best, L)
             sc0=_score(a0)
             if sc0 < best_sc: best=a0; best_sc=sc0
             for a in _make_inits(L, rng, parent=best, max_inits=6):
                sc=_score(a)
                if sc < best_sc: best=a.copy(); best_sc=sc
             endL=min(hetero_end-2, time.monotonic()+per_time)
             if endL > time.monotonic()+3 and best.size <= 768:
                # if current best length == L, optimize it; else don't change best until improves? We'll optimize a candidate and update if better.
                cand=a0 if a0.size==L else _upsample(best,L)
                cand_sc=sc0
                cand,bc=_run_optimizer(cand, time.monotonic(), endL, rng, lr_start=0.12, lr_end=0.05, alpha_start=0.4, alpha_end=0.0, focus_start=3.0, focus_end=10.0, polish_every=600, polish_evals=30, polish_time=2.0, max_lp=2)
                if bc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                    best=cand; best_sc=bc
                else:
                    # still update best for this length if lower than best? already
                    pass
                # quick polish candidate
                cand2,bc2=_quick_polish(cand, rng, max_evals=80, time_limit=3.0)
                if bc2 < best_sc - 1e-12:
                    best=cand2; best_sc=bc2
    # final validate
    candidates=[]
    if parent is not None: candidates.append(parent)
    if best is not None: candidates.append(best)
    candidates.append(np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64))
    final_list=None; final_score=np.inf
    for cand in candidates:
       try:
          arr=np.asarray(cand,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
          if arr.size==0: continue
          # sanitize and cap? nonnegative finite
          arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
          arr=np.maximum(arr,0.0)
          arr=np.minimum(arr,1000.0)
          if float(arr.sum()) < 0.01: continue
          lst=[float(x) for x in arr]
          sc=float(evaluate_sequence(lst))
          if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < final_score:
             final_score=sc; final_list=lst
       except Exception:
          pass
    if final_list is None:
       final_list=[1.0]
    return final_list
```

In hetero phase, note after optimizing candidate L, if not better than global, but `a0` from global upsample may be worse; we shouldn't set best to a0 if sc0 > best_sc. Simpler: At start of each L, set `start_arr = _upsample(best, L)` (global best at that time). Evaluate. If improves, update best. Then `cand = _upsample(best, L)` (which may be updated if best from inits length L). Use candidate for optimizer. If optimizer improves, update. Good.

for L in lengths:
